Work in finite r r r -uniform bi-hypergraphs H = ( V , E ) H=(V,E) H = ( V , E ) , i.e. mixed hypergraphs with C = D = E ⊆ ( V r ) \mathcal C=\mathcal D=E\subseteq \binom Vr C = D = E ⊆ ( r V ​ ) . A coloring c : V → N c:V\to \mathbb N c : V → N is proper if 1 < ∣ c ( e ) ∣ < r ( e ∈ E ) . 1<|c(e)|<r\qquad(e\in E). 1 < ∣ c ( e ) ∣ < r ( e ∈ E ) . Thus no edge is monochromatic and no edge is rainbow. A bi-hypergraph is minimal uncolorable if it is uncolorable, but every proper subhypergraph is colorable. Let M U C ( n , r ) \mathcal{MUC}(n,r) M U C ( n , r ) be the set of minimal uncolorable r r r -uniform bi-hypergraphs on n n n vertices. The conjecture is: ∀ r ≥ 3 , ∀ n ≥ ( r − 1 ) 2 + 1 , M U C ( n , r ) ≠ ∅ . \forall r\ge 3,\ \forall n\ge (r-1)^2+1,\qquad \mathcal{MUC}(n,r)\ne\varnothing . ∀ r ≥ 3 , ∀ n ≥ ( r − 1 ) 2 + 1 , M U C ( n , r )  = ∅ . This is exactly Conjecture 1 in Zhang--Dong--Zhang. Result: The conjecture is true. Put q = ( r − 1 ) 2 + 1 = r + ( r − 1 ) ( r − 2 ) , n = q + t , t ≥ 0. q=(r-1)^2+1=r+(r-1)(r-2), \qquad n=q+t,\quad t\ge 0. q = ( r − 1 ) 2 + 1 = r + ( r − 1 ) ( r − 2 ) , n = q + t , t ≥ 0. Let R = { z 0 , a 2 , … , a r } , R=\{z_0,a_2,\dots,a_r\}, R = { z 0 ​ , a 2 ​ , … , a r ​ } , and let W = W 1 ∪ ⋯ ∪ W r − 2 W=W_1\cup\cdots\cup W_{r-2} W = W 1 ​ ∪ ⋯ ∪ W r − 2 ​ , where the W i W_i W i ​ 's are disjoint and ∣ W i ∣ = r − 1 |W_i|=r-1 ∣ W i ​ ∣ = r − 1 . Add vertices z 1 , … , z t z_1,\dots,z_t z 1 ​ , … , z t ​ . For t = 0 t=0 t = 0 , take the complete r r r -uniform hypergraph on R ∪ W R\cup W R ∪ W . It is uncolorable by the pigeonhole principle, and deleting any vertex leaves ( r − 1 ) 2 (r-1)^2 ( r − 1 ) 2 vertices, colorable with r − 1 r-1 r − 1 color classes of size r − 1 r-1 r − 1 . Assume t ≥ 1 t\ge 1 t ≥ 1 . Define G t G_t G t ​ on V = R ∪ W ∪ { z 1 , … , z t } V=R\cup W\cup\{z_1,\dots,z_t\} V = R ∪ W ∪ { z 1 ​ , … , z t ​ } with edge set consisting of: all r r r -subsets of R ∪ W R\cup W R ∪ W , except R R R ; all sets { z i } ∪ T \{z_i\}\cup T { z i ​ } ∪ T , where 1 ≤ i ≤ t 1\le i\le t 1 ≤ i ≤ t and T ∈ ( W r − 1 ) T\in\binom W{r-1} T ∈ ( r − 1 W ​ ) ; all sets { z i − 1 , z i } ∪ T \{z_{i-1},z_i\}\cup T { z i − 1 ​ , z i ​ } ∪ T , where 1 ≤ i ≤ t 1\le i\le t 1 ≤ i ≤ t and T ∈ ( W r − 2 ) T\in\binom W{r-2} T ∈ ( r − 2 W ​ ) ; the final edge L t = { z t , a 2 , … , a r } . L_t=\{z_t,a_2,\dots,a_r\}. L t ​ = { z t ​ , a 2 ​ , … , a r ​ } . First observe that in any proper coloring of the complete r r r -graph on R ∪ W R\cup W R ∪ W with the single edge R R R removed, the vertices of R R R must be monochromatic, and W W W must split into exactly r − 2 r-2 r − 2 color classes of size r − 1 r-1 r − 1 . Indeed, if R R R were not monochromatic, then no color class could have size r r r , and no r r r distinct colors could occur; otherwise there would be a forbidden monochromatic or rainbow edge different from R R R . But then at most ( r − 1 ) 2 (r-1)^2 ( r − 1 ) 2 vertices could be colored, contradicting ∣ R ∪ W ∣ = ( r − 1 ) 2 + 1 |R\cup W|=(r-1)^2+1 ∣ R ∪ W ∣ = ( r − 1 ) 2 + 1 . Now suppose G t G_t G t ​ has a proper coloring. From the previous paragraph, R R R has one color, say α \alpha α , and W W W has r − 2 r-2 r − 2 other colors, each appearing r − 1 r-1 r − 1 times. Inductively, for each i i i , the edges { z i } ∪ T \{z_i\}\cup T { z i ​ } ∪ T forbid z i z_i z i ​ from having one of the W W W -colors, and the edges { z i − 1 , z i } ∪ T \{z_{i-1},z_i\}\cup T { z i − 1 ​ , z i ​ } ∪ T , with T T T choosing one vertex from each W j W_j W j ​ , forbid z i z_i z i ​ from having a new color. Hence every z i z_i z i ​ has color α \alpha α . Therefore L t L_t L t ​ is monochromatic, contradiction. So G t G_t G t ​ is uncolorable. Also G t − v G_t-v G t ​ − v is colorable for every vertex v v v . If v v v lies on the path z 0 , z 1 , … , z t z_0,z_1,\dots,z_t z 0 ​ , z 1 ​ , … , z t ​ , color the component before v v v and the fixed vertices a 2 , … , a r a_2,\dots,a_r a 2 ​ , … , a r ​ with one color, the component after v v v with a second color, and color each W j W_j W j ​ monochromatically with its own color. If v ∈ L t v\in L_t v ∈ L t ​ , the edge L t L_t L t ​ disappears and the same coloring with only one path-color works. If v ∈ W v\in W v ∈ W , color z 0 , … , z t , a 2 z_0,\dots,z_t,a_2 z 0 ​ , … , z t ​ , a 2 ​ with color 1 1 1 , color a 3 , … , a r a_3,\dots,a_r a 3 ​ , … , a r ​ with color 2 2 2 , and color W ∖ { v } W\setminus\{v\} W ∖ { v } using r − 3 r-3 r − 3 vertices of color 1 1 1 , one vertex of color 2 2 2 , and r − 1 r-1 r − 1 vertices in each of colors 3 , … , r − 1 3,\dots,r-1 3 , … , r − 1 . A direct check shows every remaining edge has between 2 2 2 and r − 1 r-1 r − 1 colors. Thus G t G_t G t ​ is vertex-critical uncolorable. Finally, enlarge the nonedge set of G t G_t G t ​ maximally while preserving uncolorability. Equivalently, delete edges from G t G_t G t ​ until an edge-minimal uncolorable spanning subhypergraph H t H_t H t ​ is obtained. Since every G t − v G_t-v G t ​ − v is colorable, every H t − v H_t-v H t ​ − v is colorable too. Edge-minimality gives that deleting any edge makes H t H_t H t ​ colorable. Hence every proper subhypergraph of H t H_t H t ​ is colorable, so H t ∈ M U C ( n , r ) H_t\in\mathcal{MUC}(n,r) H t ​ ∈ M U C ( n , r ) . Therefore M U C ( n , r ) ≠ ∅ \mathcal{MUC}(n,r)\ne\varnothing M U C ( n , r )  = ∅ for all r ≥ 3 r\ge3 r ≥ 3 and n ≥ ( r − 1 ) 2 + 1 n\ge(r-1)^2+1 n ≥ ( r − 1 ) 2 + 1 . Citation: No prior resolution is used here. The reconstructed conjecture and notation are from Zhang, Dong, and Zhang, “On the colorability of bi-hypergraphs,” arXiv:2310.06464, Conjecture 1. show the rest collapse Read by a language model on # 1 · not a proof model says: correct GPT-5.5 xhigh (SMD judge 1) Aug 17, 2026 scope Full solution as submitted; SMD novelty classification TYPE2 PASS The construction attacks the correct conjecture and is mathematically sound. The base complete-minus- R R R lemma correctly forces R R R to be monochromatic and W W W to split into r − 2 r-2 r − 2 classes of size r − 1 r-1 r − 1 . The added edges then force all z i z_i z i ​ to have the R R R -color, making L t L_t L t ​ monochromatic, so G t G_t G t ​ is uncolorable. The proposed vertex-deletion colorings check out, and passing to an edge-minimal uncolorable spanning subhypergraph indeed gives a minimal uncolorable r r r -uniform bi-hypergraph on n n n vertices. For t = 0 t=0 t = 0 , the complete r r r -graph is also edge-minimal by coloring the deleted edge monochromatically and partitioning the remaining vertices. A literature search found only the original paper/known r = 3 r=3 r = 3 partial results, not a prior general result of this strength. Novelty assessment TYPE2 Classification rationale: The result appears genuinely new and resolves the full stated Conjecture 1, extending the known r = 3 r=3 r = 3 constructions to all r ≥ 3 r\ge3 r ≥ 3 and all n ≥ ( r − 1 ) 2 + 1 n\ge (r-1)^2+1 n ≥ ( r − 1 ) 2 + 1 . The construction is elementary and the area is fairly specialized, so it is not TYPE3, but resolving an explicit published/open conjecture should be enough for a short standalone combinatorics note. Literature check: I found no prior source proving this general non-emptiness statement. Searches for the exact notation M U C ( n , r ) \mathcal{MUC}(n,r) M U C ( n , r ) , “minimal uncolorable bi-hypergraph(s)”, the threshold ( r − 1 ) 2 + 1 (r-1)^2+1 ( r − 1 ) 2 + 1 , and the paper title led back to the original Zhang–Dong–Zhang paper, copies/indexing pages, and older mixed-hypergraph literature. The original paper proves only the r = 3 r=3 r = 3 order-existence result and explicitly states the all- r r r assertion as Conjecture 1. A later indexed SSRN preprint by the same authors on Tuza–Voloshin’s problem appears related to minimum size questions, not a clear prior resolution of this conjecture.
